How to read the tags

Verified re-checked in this revision against the live artefact file, Linear, Vercel or a cited public source. Corrected a v1 figure that was wrong or imprecise; the new figure follows. Unverified stated by a principal or a source we could not reach; the confirming step is named. Assumption a modelled figure; the basis and the variable that drives it are named. New in v2 content that did not exist in v1.

Part 0

Executive summary

The plan in v1 was right in direction, and one thing it asserted was wrong: the site did not work end to end. Building Phase A found that the customer app never initialised at all (Appendix E). That is fixed, along with the three design phases. Re-verifying every number against the live file and the live systems changed seven things, and each of them changes what happens next. This part is the whole dossier in three pages.

Linear issues done / total, M1 due 15 Oct 2026 Verified

The seven findings that change the plan

#FindingEvidenceWhat changes
1The v1 PDF is not a usable copy of the source. Corrected351 of 3,586 lines in Part 11 of v1 were cut at the page edge with a " >>" marker. Only 218,696 of 236,498 bytes are recoverable from the PDF. The live artefact was read in full and hashed instead (Appendix A).The Git repository xrcallum/King-laundry- becomes the copy of record. Appendix C now wraps lines instead of truncating them. Every future publish is committed first.
2The emoji census was under-counted.A Unicode scan of the live file finds 83 <i> tags, 75 of which contain an emoji (29 distinct), plus 49 loose symbol glyphs in prose (16 distinct; 23 are the "→" arrow). v1 said 47 and 19.Phase A acceptance test becomes "Unicode scan returns zero", never a count. The icon sprite needs about 30 symbols, not 27 (Part 8).
3laundrykings.com.au cannot be bought through Vercel today, and laundrykings.com is taken.Vercel reports .com.au and .com "not available for purchase". DNS: laundrykings.com resolves to a third-party host; laundrykings.com.au has no record, which is consistent with either an unregistered name that Vercel does not sell, or a registered but unused name. Unverified until an auDA WHOIS lookup.M2's headline outcome "site live at laundrykings.com.au" now depends on KEA-14 (an active ABN is required for any .com.au) and on a registrar that sells .com.au. Options in Part 16, decision D-09.
4Laundry Lady's rate card is partly public, and it changes the +5% decision.Search-indexed content of thelaundrylady.com.au/pricing (effective 27 July 2026): minimum $70.00, pickup and delivery $14.50, service fee $2.20 recurring or $5.50 one-off. Their 2024 price list: $27.50 per 5 kg load, ironing from $2.75 per item. Direct fetch of the page is blocked from this environment, so the per-load 2026 figure is Unverified.Our $32 load is already about 16% above their 2024 load price; a further +5% on line items is not needed to be "premium". The stronger position is the all-in floor: a one-off Laundry Lady booking appears to cost $70 + $14.50 + $5.50 = $90.00 versus our $73.50 with delivery included, if their minimum excludes those fees. Part 3 and decision D-04.
5The Kings Club "save around $28 every week" claim is true only at two loads.At the v4.2 price: 2 loads ($64.00) + delivery ($13.50) = $77.50 versus Solo $49.00, saving $28.50 (36.8%) Verified. After the 19 September re-price (Solo $63.70) the same comparison saves $13.80 (17.8%), and at one load ($45.50 ad hoc) a Solo member pays $18.20 more.Qualify the claim with its basis wherever it appears. Australian Consumer Law treats an unqualified comparison as a representation. Done: the home page now computes the figure from live pricing and reads "save around $14 a week compared with two ad-hoc loads".
6Red on navy fails contrast at 2.07:1.Computed from the brand tokens (#CC2228 on #1A3480). The Bold Editorial direction puts red CTAs, crown and price figures on navy surfaces.Red never sits directly on navy. Red on paper or white only; on navy, the CTA is white-filled with red text, or a white outline. Written into the design system (Part 8).
7Linear is behind the dossier.KEA-11 still reads "deploy laundrykings.com.au to Cloudflare Pages" while v1 says Vercel. No issue carries a due date. Every issue is assigned to Callum, including the four decisions v1 gives to Max. Milestone dates exist only in Linear: M1 15 Oct, M2 30 Nov, M3 31 Dec 2026.Ten-minute hygiene pass (Part 6): retitle KEA-11, add due dates, add Max as a Linear member and reassign D-01, D-03, D-04 and D-07.

Everything else in one paragraph

Pricing, coverage and the data model are unchanged and were re-verified. The five root causes and fifteen defects from v1 are confirmed in the live file and six are added, mostly about scale discipline (57 distinct font sizes, 17 distinct radii) and about what the marketing site is missing for launch (no Open Graph tags, no structured data, nine aria-labels across 34 pages). The design direction stands; Part 8 turns it into a token-level specification a builder can execute without taste calls. Part 9 adds a Now/Next/Later feature roadmap built around three modern expectations a customer has of a pickup service in 2026: install it without an app store, see where the job is, and see proof of the work. Part 10 and Part 11 replace "get insurance" and "check Fair Work" with named policies, named tests and named owners. Part 16 closes with fourteen decisions, each with options and a recommendation.

Recommended sequence, next ten working days
  1. Today, Callum (19 Sep): commit the v4.2 file to Git, run the pre-flight script in Appendix A, then execute Phase A exactly as the resume block in Part 17 states. One publish.
  2. Today, Callum (19 Sep): Linear hygiene pass (Part 6) and run the auDA WHOIS check on laundrykings.com.au (Part 16, D-09).
  3. By 25 Sep, Max: pricing decision D-04 with the competitor card in Part 3; company name, ABN and address for the footer (D-03); confirm the three Kings Club price points (D-07).
  4. By 26 Sep, both: seed config/contact and config/payment (D-01) so the phone placeholder disappears and an invoice can be paid.
  5. By 30 Sep, Callum: Phase B and Phase C publishes, imagery via Gamma, then close KEA-18.
  6. By 3 Oct, Max with adviser: operator engagement brief (Part 10) covering contractor test, employee-like exposure and Super, before KEA-7 registers the first operator.

Part 1

Where we left off

Nothing has been published. The live artefact is still v4.2. Phases A, B and C are built, verified and committed to the branch, and wait on a principal to publish. The census is independently re-verified. Superseded v2 said Phase A had not started.

Exact stopping point Verified

Live artefact at v4.2, contract 0.2.52, capabilities {"db":{},"user":{}}, 3,586 lines, 236,498 bytes. The artefact service confirms the stored declaration matches. Script block parses cleanly (node --check), 281 element IDs with no duplicates, all 120 getElementById targets exist.

Next action

Open a fresh chat, paste the resume block from Part 17 (it has changed since v1), say "go". Phase A begins. Before the first publish, commit the current file to Git so there is a copy of record that is not a PDF.

The one thing to know

The product is now functionally able to take a first paid job, which it was not before this build: the booking flow was dead in v4.2 and is proven working by a browser test (Appendix E). It is still not launchable in public: the footer carries a placeholder ABN, the phone number is a placeholder, payments are a pre-filled email rather than a checkout, and the design reads as a template. The next phase is craft and launch readiness, not features. The features in Part 9 are sequenced after that, deliberately.

Part 2

The project, principals and brand

LaundryKings is laundry pickup and delivery across Brisbane, Logan and South East Queensland. Independent operators run collection rounds in their own suburb under the LaundryKings brand. Web-first, no app download. Tagline: "We Wash It Well."

Principals

PrincipalStrengths and role on this ventureOwns
Callum PageBuild, go-to-market, AI, web design, speed to market, sales. Linear team lead and the only assignee on the board today.Artefact, Git repository, Linear, Vercel, design and build decisions.
Max JonesStructure, finance, long-range planning. Prior multi-site hospitality and laundry group operator.Google Drive, pricing, entity and ABN, operator engagement model, adviser relationships.

Contact detail held in v1 (callumpageinfo@gmail.com) is personal; the working address for this venture is callum@maxcalprojects.com. Max's address is not recorded in any project document; see Part 18.

The table above is the record of what v4.2 shipped. Sampling the supplied logo gives red #D84330 and navy #0C265F, and the repository copy now uses them: --navy #0C265F, --navy-d #071A45, --red-brand #D84330 (logo, hero slab, non-text marks; 4.41:1 on white so never small text) and --red #C43828 (the same hue deepened for buttons and small text; 5.33:1 on white, 4.81:1 on paper, 14.4:1 white on navy). Part 8 and Appendix E carry the detail.

Fonts: Archivo for headings (500 to 900), Inter for body (400 to 600), from Google Fonts. Australian English throughout. Every displayed price is AUD and GST-inclusive.

Voice and tone New in v2

Say

Short declaratives. Price before adjective. "Booked. Collected Tuesday 9 to 12. Back Wednesday." Use "your operator", never "our driver". Use "we" for the brand promise and "your operator" for the person at the door.

Do not say

"Premium", "hassle-free", "best in Brisbane", any income claim for operators, any turnaround we cannot guarantee, any comparison without its basis. No exclamation marks. No emoji.

Compliance voice

State the limit plainly and once: "We are not a registered NDIS provider." "We do not hold AS/NZS 4146 accreditation, so we do not take clinical linen." The site already does this well; keep it.

Part 3

Pricing, competitor rate card, unit economics, coverage and booking

Source of truth for price is the database document config/pricing. These figures were re-read from the live file and are unchanged from v1. What is new is the competitor card with dates and sources, a parametric unit-economics model, and a qualifier on the Kings Club savings claim.

Rate card, AUD, GST-inclusive Verified

ItemPrice
Wash and fold, per load (about 5 kg)$32.00
Iron, per item$3.50
Trouser press$4.50
Collection and return$13.50
Minimum per collection (one-off bookings)$73.50
Eco or sensitive-skin add-on, per load$3.50
ItemPrice
Dry clean: jacket / dress / suit$18.50 / $16.50 / $32.00
Bulk: doona / king / curtain$45.00 / $75.00 / $38.00
Large linen: sheet set / blanket or quilt cover / rug, per item (replaces ironing, 19 Sep 2026)$24.00 / $32.00 / $42.00
Kings Club Solo (about 1 to 2 loads), per week — $49.00 + 30%, 19 Sep 2026$63.70
Kings Club Household (about 2 to 4 loads), per week — $69.00 + 30%$89.70
Kings Club Full House (about 4 to 6 loads), per week — $89.00 + 30%$115.70
Kings Club membersexempt from minimum

Invoice maths in the ops portal: loads = ceil(kg ÷ 5) × $32.00; large linen = items × $24.00 at the sheet-set rate, with blankets (+$8) and rugs (+$18) entered through the adjustment field until the wizard takes separate counts; plus $13.50 delivery; floor at $73.50; a manual adjustment field. The minimum was set at Laundry Lady's $70 plus 5% on 18 September 2026.

Kings Club savings claim: add the basis New in v2

Two ad-hoc loads a week cost $64.00 + $13.50 = $77.50. At the current Solo price of $63.70 the saving is $13.80 a week, 17.8%; at one load the ad-hoc price is $45.50, so Solo costs $18.20 more. Under the Australian Consumer Law an unqualified comparison is a representation the customer can rely on, so the copy carries its basis: "save around $14 a week compared with two ad-hoc loads". The figure is computed from config/pricing at render time and rounded to whole dollars, so a future re-price cannot leave a stale number in the sentence. Done 19 Sep 2026 (Appendix E).

Figure 3.1. The comparison that matters to a first-time customer is the all-in floor, not the per-load rate. Segment widths are to scale; the Laundry Lady structure is read from their pricing page and needs Max's confirmation.

Recommendation on the +5% decision (D-04)

Keep line-item prices where they are. Do not apply +5% across the card. Reposition the comparison from "minimum" to "all-in floor with delivery included and no service fee". Confirm two facts first: Laundry Lady's 2026 per-load price, and whether their $70 minimum includes the delivery fee. Owner Max, due 25 Sep 2026. Options with reasoning are in Part 16.

Unit economics per booking: parametric model Assumption New in v2

No operator payout has been set, so no margin figure exists. The model below is a formula with its variables named; the illustrative column uses a two-load, no-ironing, card-paid, one-off booking. Change the variables in the Ops Sheet and the row values follow. None of the illustrative figures is a forecast.

LineFormula or basisIllustrative, 2 loadsStatus
Revenue, inc. GST2 × $32.00 + $13.50$77.50Rate card

Revenue, ex GSTRevenue − GST$70.45

Operator payoutVariable P = share of ex-GST revenue, or a fixed fee per load plus a per-collection fee. Not set. Options in Part 10.PVariable

Part 4

Architecture, data model and the production target

One HTML file, 34 hash-routed sections, one script block of 77,542 characters, no build step, no server. That is a strength for speed and a constraint for launch. This part records both, and names the production shape the roadmap moves toward.

Figure 4.1. Three surfaces, one file, one database. Every page is a <section class="page">; 34 counted in the live file.

Job lifecycle Verified

Figure 4.2. Five live states and one terminal exception. Two proposed states give the customer the two moments they most want to see: "your laundry is being washed" and "it is back at your door".

Ops workflow today, no server Verified

  1. Customer books in the wizard; a document is written to bookings_inbox.
  2. Ops opens #/ops, assigns an operator from operators. "Email customer" opens a pre-filled Gmail draft; "Add to Calendar" opens a pre-filled Google Calendar event.
  3. Operator collects; ops marks collected.
  4. Ops opens #/opsinv, enters weight and, for a large-linen job, the item count; the invoice calculates with the minimum and any manual adjustment; a pre-filled invoice email opens; ops confirms and later marks paid.

Payment today is whatever config/payment says: PayID or BSB text and a Stripe payment link pasted into the email. That is workable for the first jobs and is not a checkout. KEA-13 replaces it.

Security and access model Verified

ConcernTodayBefore public launch
Ops gatingOps pages render only when CAN_EDIT is true, derived from the artefact's editor status.Adequate while ops is the two principals. Not adequate once operators need their own view (KEA-16).
Customer identityArtefact user capability; a signed-in viewer gets a persisted private store, an anonymous viewer gets a session-only store with a visible notice.Public visitors are anonymous, so bookings from the public site land in the shared inbox without an authenticated owner. Fine for M1, not for M2.
Personal informationNames, addresses, phone, email and care notes in the shared inbox are readable by any editor.Least-privilege rules on a real backend; retention rule already written in the privacy draft (five years, applications twelve months).
Payment dataNone stored. Correct.Keep it that way: Stripe Checkout hosted page, never card fields on our page.

Production target for M2 New in v2

The dossier v1 recorded the choice of Vercel over Cloudflare Pages; Linear still says Cloudflare (Part 6). The shape below is the smallest production system that keeps the single-file front end intact, which protects the convergence rule and the build velocity.

LayerChoiceWhy this, why not the alternativeLinear
Hosting and functionsVercel (static file + serverless functions)Already connected. Functions give us a place for Stripe webhooks and email sends without a server to run. Cloudflare Pages would work equally; the decision is made, so Linear should follow it.KEA-11
Database and authSupabase (Postgres + Auth + Row Level Security) RecommendedFirebase is the closer match to the current document model, so migration is easier there. Supabase wins on SQL for finance reporting, on row-level rules for operators, and on Australian region hosting (Sydney). Either replaces the artefact db and user capabilities. Decision D-10.KEA-11, KEA-16
PaymentsStripe Checkout + Stripe Billing for Kings ClubHosted checkout, Apple Pay and Google Pay without extra integration, subscriptions with pause and skip built in. 1.7% + $0.30 domestic, dropping to 1.65% from 1 Oct 2026.KEA-13
EmailResend (transactional)Templates for booked, assigned, collected, invoiced, paid, returned. Postmark is the equivalent; pick one and stop.KEA-12
SMSTwilio or MessageMedia (AU sender ID)Only for the two moments customers act on: "operator is 15 minutes away" and "invoice ready". Spam Act consent captured at booking (Part 11).new
Domain and DNSSee D-09.com.au requires an active ABN under auDA rules; .au direct requires Australian presence only.KEA-11, KEA-14

What the live file contains that v1 did not list Verified

  • A written customer guarantee: re-wash within 48 hours, delivery fee refunded on late return, damage and loss claims within 7 days with a 5-business-day investigation, settlement at current item value, declared exclusions (missing labels, pre-existing damage, undeclared colour-bleed risk, pocket contents, undeclared items over $250), and an explicit statement that ACL rights are not limited.
  • Privacy Policy and Terms of Service marked "Draft for legal review, not yet in force" with a blank date of effect. Both must be reviewed by a solicitor before M2 (Part 11).
  • Operator page states plainly that no income figures are published because there is no genuine earnings data yet. Keep this; it is the right posture under the ACL and the right posture for recruiting.
  • Dry cleaning is described as via partner cleaners, PERC-free. This is a supplier claim; hold the partner's confirmation on file (Part 11).

Five root causes, confirmed

RootCauseEvidence in live file
R1No optical scale13 tracking values, 57 font sizes, 17 radii, one border weight everywhere. Nothing changes with size, so large type looks cramped and small type looks loose.
R2Transparency used for state.plan.on lays 13% red over navy and produces indigo. Any rgba over a coloured surface will drift off-brand.
R3Borders below 1.3:1Cards float without edges; the eye reads the shadow instead, which is why the page feels like a template.
R4Flat lists where hierarchy is neededFooter 26 equal links, 15-item mobile menu, 16 identical suburb chips, filter chips all the same weight.
R5Emoji as icon system75 in tags, 49 loose. Renders as iOS or Android colour emoji, different on each device, wrong optical size, no stroke consistency.

Figure 7.1. Phase A fixes the tokens, so nine defects fall out of four root causes at once. Phase B is hierarchy. Phase C is the app shell.

IDDefectRootPhaseAcceptance test
D1Tab bar mixes glyph and colour emoji; renders as iOS emoji. Worst single defect.R5CTab bar uses 5 sprite icons; zero emoji in file.

Part 8

Design system specification: Bold Editorial

Confident, graphic, unapologetically LaundryKings. Own the crown, own the red. Differentiate hard from Laundry Lady's soft pink domestic look. Polish benchmark is Rinse (US). v1 gave the direction; this part gives the tokens, so a build session makes no taste calls.

Type scale, 1.25 major third, 8 steps

Exact ratio from a 13 px base gives 13 / 16.25 / 20.3 / 25.4 / 31.7 / 39.7 / 49.6 / 62.0. The tokens round to whole pixels and round the top four down (31, 39, 49, 61) so headings sit slightly tighter than the pure ratio; this is a deliberate optical choice carried over from v1, not an arithmetic error.

TokenSizeTrackingWeightLeadingUse
--fs-display61 px · clamp(2.8rem, 6.5vw, 5.4rem) for H1−0.04em9001.0Home hero only
--fs-149 px−0.03em9001.05Page H1
--fs-239 px−0.03em8001.1Section heads, how-it-works numerals at 4rem
--fs-331 px−0.02em8001.15Card titles on home, price figures
--fs-425 px−0.02em7001.2H2 in app, wizard step title
--fs-520 px−0.01em7001.3H3, tab labels on tablet
--fs-body16 px0400/5001.55All body copy; never below 16 px in inputs (iOS zoom)
--fs-micro13 px+0.01em · caps +0.08em500/6001.4Captions, eyebrows, chips, tab labels

Figure 8.1. Tracking loosens as size drops. Rendered at 60% of screen size (1 px = 0.6 pt here) so the largest step fits the print column; ratios between steps are exact. Weight 900 is Archivo Black-equivalent and is reserved for the two largest steps.

Colour roles and state colours

RoleTokenRule
Paper surface (60%+ of any page)--paper #F6F3EEReplaces #EEF0F8 and most of bg2. Warm, not blue-grey. Cards on paper are white; cards on white are paper. No hairline needed when the surface steps.
Navy structure--navy-d #071A45 page · --navy #0C265F raised · --navy-sel #2A2F6B selectedThree solid tints, never rgba. Hero and footer sit on navy-d. Selected plan card is #2A2F6B solid (D3).
Red accent (under 5% of surface)--red #C43828 text and CTAs · --red-brand #D84330 logo and display slabs · --red-d #A82E20 hoverBoth reds are the logo's hue (sampled #D84330). The true logo red is 4.41:1 on white, so it is reserved for the logo, display-size text and non-text marks; --red is the same hue deepened to clear AA for small text and white-on-red buttons (5.33:1 on white, 4.81:1 on paper). One full-bleed slab per page at most. Never directly on navy (D16).
CTA on navywhite fill, red textWhite #FFFFFF button with red text gives 5.33:1; or white outline with white text at 14.4:1 on navy. Hover: paper fill.
Hover and selected on navy--navy-hov #23407A · --navy-sel #2A2F6BSolid. Focus ring is 2 px #FFFFFF offset 2 px on navy, 2 px #0C265F on light surfaces.
Elevation and motion--e-hov · --e-hov-dk · --e-press · --e-sheet · --e-toast · --lift -3px · --ease · --ease-spring · --tr .18s · --tr-page .36sDepth is an interaction state, never a resting one (D15): a lift and soft shadow on hover, a scale-down on press. One easing curve for everything that moves, a spring only for toggles, steppers and the FAQ icon. Route, wizard-step and menu entrances share one fade-and-slide; reduced motion zeroes the page transition and the global rule kills the rest. Added 19 Sep 2026, Appendix E.
Text--ink #141826 · --body #4A5266 · --mute #6B7386Mute darkened from #7C8598 to #6B7386, 4.75:1 on white (D20). On navy-d: white at 14.7:1, or #C9D2F2 for secondary at 9.8:1.
Borders1px rgba(20,24,38,.08) on white · none on paperOr a surface step. Drop shadows removed except the sticky tab bar and open sheets.
Statusok #0F7B4F/#E6F6EE · warn #8A5A00/#FFF6E0 · bad #A81B20/#FFF1F1Always with an icon and a word. Never colour alone. Never reused as decoration.

Colour ratio warning, restated

Navy + red + paper reads "sporting club" the moment red exceeds about 5% of the visible surface or sits on navy. Paper carries the page. Navy gives structure. Red is a signal. If a screen has more than one red element above the fold besides the crown, one of them is wrong.

Spacing, radius, elevation, motion

Spacing, 4 px base

--sp-1 4 · --sp-2 8 · --sp-3 12 · --sp-4 16 · --sp-5 24 · --sp-6 32 · --sp-7 48 · --sp-8 64 · --sp-9 96

Section rhythm clamp(3.5rem, 7vw, 5.5rem) stays. Card padding is sp-5 on desktop, sp-4 on mobile. Nothing uses a value outside this list.

Radius, three tokens

--r 6 (inputs, chips) · --r2 12 (cards) · --r3 18 (sheets, hero panels) · 999 px pills · 50% avatars

Elevation

Level 0 surface step only. Level 1 sticky bars: 0 1px 0 rgba(20,24,38,.08). Level 2 sheets and menus: 0 18px 50px rgba(20,24,38,.16). No shadow on cards.

Motion

Fade-up 12 px over 320 ms ease-out on scroll entry, once per element. Tab switch 180 ms. Everything inside @media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference); the reduced-motion branch shows the final state instantly.

Icon sprite, one set, 24 px grid, 1.75 stroke

One inline <svg> with <symbol> elements at the top of the body; each use is <svg class="ic"><use href="#ic-truck"/></svg>. Stroke uses currentColor so icons inherit text colour and pass wherever the text passes. The list below is derived from the emoji census and covers every current use plus the tab bar.

ReplacesSymbol IDs (30)
Tab bar (5)ic-home · ic-bookings (list) · ic-book (plus in circle) · ic-messages (chat) · ic-settings (sliders)
Service and flow (10)ic-truck · ic-basket · ic-shirt · ic-iron · ic-hanger · ic-scale · ic-box · ic-calendar · ic-clock · ic-pin
Account and money (8)ic-user · ic-users · ic-receipt · ic-card · ic-bank · ic-doc · ic-lock · ic-shield
System (7)ic-check · ic-x · ic-arrow-right · ic-refresh · ic-info · ic-alert · ic-help

Add ic-crown as the one brand mark, drawn at 24 and 48 px. Icons are always accompanied by a text label or an aria-label (D19). Optical sizes: 20 px in chips and inputs, 24 px default, 28 px in the tab bar.

Imagery brief New in v2

Every strong competitor shows a human and a price in three seconds. We show a price. Phase B adds the human with generated placeholders; M2 replaces them with a real operator, photographed once, half a day, in their own suburb. Brief for both:

  • Subject: one operator, mid-thirties to fifties, in a plain navy tee, at a real Logan or Brisbane-south front door, handing over a folded stack in a LaundryKings bag. Daylight, overcast preferred. No studio, no stock smile.
  • Crop and ratio: hero 4:5 portrait and 16:9 landscape variants; how-it-works three squares (book on phone, collection at door, folded return); OG card 1200 × 630 with paper background, crown, and the line "Laundry collected, washed and returned. From $32 a load."
  • Colour grade: warm neutral to sit on paper; no pink, no teal. Red appears only as the bag tag.
  • Consent: model release signed by the operator, filed PPL08, before publication.

What is deliberately not recommended

A native app (the web-first promise is the differentiator). Live map tracking of the van (cost and privacy for operators; the 15-minute SMS is the moment that matters). Same-day service messaging until a round can actually do it. A chatbot on the marketing site. Dark mode on the marketing site (paper is the brand). Any second build platform.

Part 10

Operator model, onboarding, WHS and insurance

Operators are independent businesses with their own ABN, equipment, premises and vehicle, working an assigned suburb under the brand. That model is stated clearly on the site. What is not yet written down is how an operator is engaged, paid, protected and kept safe. This part supplies the options and the brief for the adviser. It is not legal, tax or structuring advice.

Engagement model: what the adviser must confirm before KEA-7

TestWhy it bites hereWhat we put in the brief
Contractor versus employee (common law, multi-factor)Brand sets the price, allocates the customer, routes and schedules the work, handles invoicing and support. Operators supply equipment and premises and can decline jobs. The control indicators pull both ways.The draft operator agreement, the ops workflow (Part 4), a description of who sets price, hours and territory, whether operators may work for others and subcontract.
Superannuation guarantee for contractorsA contract that is wholly or principally for labour attracts Super even with an ABN. Payment per load for washing done by the operator with their own machines mixes labour with plant.Ask: is a per-load fee "principally for labour"? Does a fee structure that separates equipment use from labour change the answer?
Fair Work "employee-like worker" (Fair Work Act s15P, in force from 26 Aug 2024)Applies to individuals performing digital platform work through a digital labour platform who have two or more of: low bargaining power, pay at or below comparable employee rates, low authority over how work is done. A web platform that allocates jobs and processes payment can be a digital labour platform. The first minimum standards order (17 Aug 2026, $31.30 to $32.00 an hour) covers on-demand delivery of food, drinks, alcohol and groceries only, so laundry is not inside it today; the deactivation code (26 Feb 2025) and unfair-deactivation remedies are not limited to that order.Ask: is LaundryKings a digital labour platform as defined? If yes, which obligations apply now (deactivation process, record-keeping) and what changes if a laundry or general last-mile order is made? Sources in Appendix B.
Payroll tax grouping, QueenslandThreshold $1.3 million of Australian taxable wages for 2026-27, rate 4.75% up to $6.5 million and 4.95% above; the deduction tapers by $1 for every $7 above the threshold and reaches nil at $10.4 million. Contractor payments can be deemed wages under the relevant-contract provisions; grouping with a principal's other entities can be triggered by common control. Not an issue at launch volumes; a design issue for the structure.Ask: are the venture entity and any related entities grouped, and would operator payments be deemed wages?

Legal and tax boundary

This dossier produces options and a brief. The decision on engagement form is the adviser's to advise and the principals' to make. Owner Max, brief sent by 26 Sep 2026, answer needed before KEA-7 (3 Oct). Draft brief text is in Part 18.

Payout model options (D-12)

OptionMechanicsForAgainst
A. Per-load fee + per-collection fee RecommendedFixed AUD per 5 kg load washed plus fixed AUD per collection round-trip. Both figures set by Max in the Ops Sheet; the ops portal calculates payout beside the invoice.Transparent, easy to model, separates plant-and-utilities cost from travel; supports the Super analysis by separating components.Needs two numbers set well; under-priced collections punish spread-out suburbs.
B. Revenue shareFixed percentage of ex-GST revenue per booking.Simplest to explain; scales with large linen and bulk items automatically.Looks most like wages; hides the cost structure; the +5% pricing debate becomes an operator-pay debate.
C. Hybrid with Kings Club retainerOption A for ad-hoc, plus a weekly fixed amount per active Kings Club customer on the round.Rewards operators for retention, which is the business model.More moving parts than the first three months need.

No payout figure is proposed here because none can be sourced. The variable P in Part 3 is where it lands. Settlement: weekly, by batch payment (PayID or ABA file) against a recipient-created tax invoice if the operator is GST-registered; the ops portal exports the batch.

WHS including chemicals New in v2

An operator's home laundry is a workplace while work is done there, and LaundryKings, as the business that engages them and directs the work, holds a primary duty of care alongside the operator under the Work Health and Safety Act 2011 (Qld). Detergents, stain removers and sanitisers are hazardous chemicals under the WHS Regulation 2011 (Qld) where the Safety Data Sheet says so.

  • Chemical register: a one-page list of approved products with the SDS for each, in OPS03; operators use only listed products. Fragrance-free and eco options are specified here, not chosen ad hoc.
  • Storage and handling: original containers, closed, off the floor, away from children; gloves for stain treatment; never mix chlorine bleach with ammonia-based products; eye-wash access.
  • Fire: dryer lint cleared every load; no overnight unattended dryer runs; smoke alarm in or near the laundry.
  • Manual handling and driving: bag weight limit, trolley for multi-bag collections, no collections after 7 pm, fatigue rule for operators running more than one round.
  • Incident reporting: in-app "report an incident" on the operator view (M3); until then, a form in OPS03 and a phone call to Callum the same day. Notifiable incidents go to Workplace Health and Safety Queensland.

Public and products liabilityBrand and each operatorInjury or property damage to third parties at the door or from the service. Brand cover should include contractors' vicarious liability.Market norm $10 million to $20 million; broker to advise.
Goods in care, custody and control (bailee's cover)BrandCustomer laundry lost, damaged, stolen or destroyed while collected, in process or in transit. Standard liability policies exclude property in your care, so this is the one that matches the written guarantee.Limit per event and per item; align with the $250 undeclared-item exclusion.

Part 16

Decision log

Fourteen decisions. Each has options with the recommended option first, an owner and a date. Decisions marked adviser produce a brief, not a conclusion.

D-01 · Seed config: phone, email, PayID or BSB, Stripe link · Both · 26 Sep 2026

  1. (Recommended) A business mobile on a brand SIM forwarded to Callum, hello@ on the chosen domain (or a Google Workspace alias now), PayID on the entity's account, Stripe payment link in test until KEA-13. Removes the placeholder and makes the first invoice payable.
  2. Personal numbers and addresses now, swap later. Faster; leaks personal details into public pages and into customer phones.

D-02 · First operator · Callum · 3 Oct 2026

  1. (Recommended) One operator in Meadowbrook or Springwood (highest live density), engaged under the adviser-cleared agreement, onboarding checklist complete, live suburbs trimmed to what that operator can reach inside the windows.
  2. Principal runs the first jobs personally under the brand. Fastest learning; blurs the operator model and delays the classification test.

D-03 · Entity name, ABN, address for footer, terms, Stripe, domain · Max · 25 Sep 2026

  1. (Recommended) Confirm the trading entity and provide the three strings. The dossier never invents them. Everything in M2 depends on this.
  2. Trade under a principal's existing ABN as a business name in the interim. Adviser question: does that create grouping or liability exposure? Options only; route to adviser.

D-04 · +5% on minimum only, or on every line · Max · 25 Sep 2026

  1. (Recommended) Keep line items as they are. Reposition on all-in floor with delivery included and no service fee. Confirm Laundry Lady's 2026 per-load price and whether their minimum includes delivery, then publish the comparison with its date.
  2. +5% on every line. Simple rule; pushes the load price to $33.60 against a competitor load price that already appears lower; no evidence customers value the premium yet.
  3. Match Laundry Lady's minimum at $70 and compete on fees. Loses the "priced above, delivers more" story the guarantee supports.

D-05 · Hero imagery: Gamma, shoot, or illustration · Callum · before Phase B (23 Sep)

  1. (Recommended) Gamma-generated placeholders in Phase B against the brief in Part 8, replaced by a half-day shoot of the first operator for M2 (KEA-15).
  2. Illustration throughout. Distinctive; lands closer to "template" than "trusted", and every competitor with traction shows a person.
  3. Shoot first. Best result; blocks Phase B on scheduling.

D-06 · Same-day messaging · Both · when true

  1. (Recommended) No same-day claim until a live round has delivered same-day for four consecutive weeks. Next-day is the promise.
  2. "Same-day where available." Vague claims invite ACL scrutiny and support load.

D-07 · Kings Club $49 / $69 / $89 final, or modelled · Max · 25 Sep 2026

  1. (Recommended) Hold the three points for launch with the two-load qualifier and a "launch pricing under review" note (already in the file). Model against real loads per member after eight weeks.
  2. Re-model now. No data yet; the model would be assumptions on assumptions.

D-08 · Nav from 6 items to 4 · Callum · Phase B

  1. (Recommended) Services · Pricing · Kings Club · For business, plus the crown home link and one CTA "Book a collection". Everything else lives in the footer and the mobile "More".
  2. Keep six. The bar competes with the logo on mobile (D14).

D-09 · Domain · Callum · 19 Sep check, 25 Sep decision

Findings: Vercel cannot sell laundrykings.com.au and laundrykings.com is registered to a third party (resolves to a hosting IP). laundrykings.au is available at USD 23.99 a year and laundrykings.co at USD 29.99 through Vercel. laundrykings.com.au has no DNS record. Any .com.au needs an active ABN with a connection to the name (D-03).

  1. (Recommended) Run an auDA WHOIS lookup today. If laundrykings.com.au is unregistered, register it through an Australian registrar in the entity's name once D-03 lands, and point it at Vercel. Also register laundrykings.au (the .au direct is cheap, defensive and needs only Australian presence) and redirect it to .com.au.
  2. If .com.au is taken: launch on laundrykings.au as the primary. Australians increasingly recognise .au direct; it needs no ABN and can be bought today. Keep the brand copy domain-free.
  3. Approach the .com holder to buy. Slow and uncertain; only if the .com.au is also gone.

Do not buy anything without Callum's explicit approval in a chat; the Vercel connector can purchase.

D-10 · Production backend: Supabase or Firebase · Callum · 14 Oct 2026 (before M2 build)

  1. (Recommended) Supabase, Sydney region: Postgres for finance reporting, row-level security for operators, auth with magic links. One-time migration of the document model into tables.
  2. Firebase: nearest to the current document model, quickest migration; weaker reporting, rules harder to audit, data region choice narrower.

D-11 · Copy of record · Callum · 19 Sep 2026

  1. (Recommended) Git repository xrcallum/King-laundry-. Every publish is committed first with the artefact version in the message. The dossier PDF references the commit hash. Appendix C remains for a paper reader.
  2. PDF as record. Proven unrecoverable (351 truncated lines).

D-12 · Operator payout model · Max · 3 Oct 2026, with adviser answer

  1. (Recommended) Option A in Part 10: per-load fee plus per-collection fee, figures set in the Ops Sheet, weekly settlement by batch. Keeps plant and labour components visible for the Super analysis.
  2. Revenue share. Simpler; looks most like wages.
  3. Hybrid with Kings Club retainer. Revisit at M3.

D-13 · Linear membership and ownership · Callum · 19 Sep 2026

  1. (Recommended) Add Max to the workspace; assign D-03, D-04, D-07, D-12, KEA-14 and the new KEA-22 and KEA-23 to Max; add due dates to every open issue.
  2. Keep Callum as sole assignee and track Max's items in this dossier. Two sources of truth.

D-14 · Dossier cadence · Both · standing

  1. (Recommended) Revise at each milestone (M1, M2, M3) and after any decision here changes. Parts 0, 1, 6, 16 and 17 update; the rest only when facts change. Superseded versions to Archive99.
  2. Revise per session. Too frequent; the Build Log covers session detail.

   Remove all 13 ad-hoc letter-spacing values.

   Replace all 57 font-size values with the 8 tokens.

   Navy tints #071A45 page / #0C265F raised / #22409A selected. No rgba over colour.

   font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums on every price.

   minimal service worker, one-time install sheet after first booking.

Method notes

  • Emoji scan class: U+1F000 to U+1FAFF, U+2600 to U+27BF, U+2B00 to U+2BFF, U+2190 to U+21FF, with optional U+FE0F. v1's lower count likely came from a narrower class or from text extracted from the PDF, where the 6 pt code font drops emoji glyphs (the PDF-derived reconstruction finds only 16). The acceptance test in the pre-flight script is zero glyphs in the whole file, so the class is deliberately wide.
  • Contrast: WCAG 2.x relative luminance, sRGB, ratio (L1 + 0.05) ÷ (L2 + 0.05). "Derived" values in Part 2 use the same formula on token pairs not directly in v1.
  • DNS: getent ahosts from the build container; HTTP probing to the candidate domains is blocked by the egress proxy, so hosting could not be inspected.
  • What could not be verified: the Gamma deck contents, the Drive documents, Laundry Lady's 2026 per-load price and whether their minimum includes delivery, and the v1 real-device screenshots (accepted as stated). The mobile menu count was verified in the fact-check pass (Appendix D).

Pre-flight script (also at verify/preflight.py in the repository)

Run before every publish. Pass --strict-tokens after Phase A lands so the token rules are enforced too.

python3 verify/preflight.py site/laundrykings-site.html --strict-tokens
# expected on v4.2 today (verified 19 Sep): FAIL on emoji (139 glyphs, 39 distinct, incl. 15 variation selectors),
# FAIL on 235 font-size and 19 letter-spacing literals; PASS on script, ids, sections, targets, sentinels
# expected after Phase A: PASS on all seven checks
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""LaundryKings pre-publish checks.

Usage:  python3 verify/preflight.py site/laundrykings-site.html [--strict-tokens]

Exit code 0 = safe to publish. Every failing check is printed with a count so the
fix is measurable. Run it before EVERY publish of the live artefact.

Checks (Dossier v2, Appendix A):
  1. exactly one <script> block and it parses (node --check)
  2. no duplicate id="" attributes
  3. exactly 34 <section class="page"> elements
  4. every getElementById('x') target exists as an id
  5. zero emoji / pictographic glyphs anywhere in the file
  6. (--strict-tokens) no font-size or letter-spacing literal outside the design tokens
  7. every function called in the script is defined
  8. AU compliance sentinels present
"""
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import collections
import os

EXPECTED_SECTIONS = 34

# Pictographs, dingbats, arrows, misc symbols, variation selectors.
EMOJI = re.compile(
    "[\U0001F000-\U0001FAFF☀-➿⬀-⯿←-⇿⌀-⏿✀-➿️‍]"
)

# Tokens allowed once Phase A has landed. Adjust here, nowhere else.
ALLOWED_TRACKING = {"-0.04em", "-.04em", "-0.03em", "-.03em", "-0.02em", "-.02em", "-0.01em", "-.01em",
                    "0", "0em", "0.01em", ".01em", "0.08em", ".08em"}
ALLOWED_FONT_SIZE = {"13px", "16px", "20px", "25px", "31px", "39px", "49px", "61px",
                     "var(--fs-micro)", "var(--fs-body)", "var(--fs-5)", "var(--fs-4)", "var(--fs-3)",
                     "var(--fs-2)", "var(--fs-1)", "var(--fs-display)"}

# Anything the browser or the artefact runtime supplies.
JS_GLOBALS = set("""
if for while switch catch function return typeof new delete void do else try finally await async
parseInt parseFloat isNaN isFinite encodeURIComponent decodeURIComponent encodeURI decodeURI
setTimeout setInterval clearTimeout clearInterval requestAnimationFrame queueMicrotask
alert confirm prompt fetch atob btoa structuredClone eval
""".split())

COMPLIANCE_SENTINELS = [
    "NDIS",                 # support page statement
    "AS/NZS 4146",          # no clinical linen
    "ABN",                  # operators page + footer
    "GST",                  # inclusive pricing
    "Australian Consumer Law",
]



def strip_js(js):
    """Blank out comments, string/template literals and regex literals, so a name
    that appears inside prose, CSS text or a character class is never mistaken
    for a function call.

    Template literals are handled with a depth counter so that nested templates
    (backtick inside ${...} inside backtick) are correctly consumed.
    """
    out, i, n, prev = [], 0, len(js), ""
    while i < n:
        c = js[i]
        two = js[i:i + 2]
        if two == "/*":
            j = js.find("*/", i + 2)
            i = n if j < 0 else j + 2
            continue
        if two == "//":
            j = js.find("\n", i)
            i = n if j < 0 else j
            continue
        if c == "`":
            # Template literal — scan with brace depth tracking so nested
            # ${`...`} structures are consumed rather than breaking the scan.
            j, depth = i + 1, 0
            while j < n:
                ch = js[j]
                if ch == "\\":
                    j += 2
                    continue
                if ch == "$" and j + 1 < n and js[j + 1] == "{":
                    depth += 1
                    j += 2
                    continue
                if ch == "}" and depth > 0:
                    depth -= 1
                    j += 1
                    continue
                if ch == "`" and depth == 0:
                    break
                j += 1
            out.append(' "" ')
            i = j + 1
            prev = ")"
            continue
        if c in "'\"":
            q, j = c, i + 1
            while j < n:
                if js[j] == "\\":
                    j += 2
                    continue
                if js[j] == q:
                    break
                j += 1
            out.append(' "" ')
            i = j + 1
            prev = ")"
            continue
        if c == "/" and prev in "(,=:[!&|?{};+*%~^<>" :
            j, klass = i + 1, False
            while j < n:
                ch = js[j]
                if ch == "\\":
                    j += 2
                    continue
                if ch == "[":
                    klass = True
                elif ch == "]":
                    klass = False
                elif ch == "/" and not klass:
                    break
                elif ch == "\n":
                    break
                j += 1
            out.append(" RX ")
            i = j + 1
            prev = ")"
            continue
        out.append(c)
        if not c.isspace():
            prev = c
        i += 1
    return "".join(out)


def main(path, strict_tokens=False):
    src = open(path, encoding="utf-8").read()
    fails = []
    notes = []

    # 1. script parses
    # Only executable blocks count; application/ld+json is data, not code.
    scripts = [m.group(2) for m in re.finditer(r"<script\b([^>]*)>(.*?)</script>", src, re.S)
               if not re.search(r'type\s*=\s*["\']application/ld\+json["\']', m.group(1))]
    if len(scripts) != 1:
        fails.append(f"expected 1 <script> block, found {len(scripts)}")
    for i, s in enumerate(scripts):
        with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile("w", suffix=".js", delete=False, encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(s)
            tmp = f.name
        r = subprocess.run(["node", "--check", tmp], capture_output=True, text=True)
        os.unlink(tmp)
        if r.returncode != 0:
            fails.append(f"script block {i} does not parse:\n{r.stderr.strip()[:800]}")
        else:
            notes.append(f"script block {i}: {len(s):,} chars, parses")

    # 2. duplicate ids
    ids = re.findall(r'\bid="([^"]+)"', src)
    dups = [k for k, v in collections.Counter(ids).items() if v > 1]
    if dups:
        fails.append(f"duplicate ids: {dups}")
    notes.append(f"{len(ids)} ids, {len(set(ids))} unique")

    # 3. sections
    n_sec = len(re.findall(r'<section class="page', src))
    if n_sec != EXPECTED_SECTIONS:
        fails.append(f"expected {EXPECTED_SECTIONS} page sections, found {n_sec}")
    else:
        notes.append(f"{n_sec} page sections")

    # 4. getElementById targets
    targets = set(re.findall(r"getElementById\(\s*['\"]([^'\"]+)['\"]\s*\)", src))
    missing = sorted(t for t in targets if t not in set(ids))
    if missing:
        fails.append(f"getElementById targets with no element: {missing}")
    notes.append(f"{len(targets)} getElementById targets, {len(missing)} missing")

    # 5. zero emoji
    found = EMOJI.findall(src)
    if found:
        c = collections.Counter(found).most_common(12)
        fails.append(f"{len(found)} emoji/pictographic glyphs remain ({len(set(found))} distinct). Top: {c}")
    else:
        notes.append("0 emoji glyphs")

    # 6. tokens
    if strict_tokens:
        # Stop at a quote too: inline style="" attributes are not brace-delimited.
        fs = [v for v in re.findall(r"font-size:\s*([^;}\"']+)", src)
              if v.strip() not in ALLOWED_FONT_SIZE and v.strip() != "inherit"
              and not v.strip().startswith("clamp(") and not v.strip().startswith("var(--fs-")]
        ls = [v for v in re.findall(r"letter-spacing:\s*([^;}\"']+)", src)
              if v.strip() not in ALLOWED_TRACKING and not v.strip().startswith("var(--track-")
              and not v.strip().startswith("var(")]
        if fs:
            fails.append(f"{len(fs)} font-size literals outside tokens: {sorted(set(v.strip() for v in fs))[:20]}")
        if ls:
            fails.append(f"{len(ls)} letter-spacing literals outside tokens: {sorted(set(v.strip() for v in ls))}")
        if not fs and not ls:
            notes.append("all font-size and letter-spacing values are tokens")

    # 7. every function called is defined
    raw = scripts[0] if scripts else ""
    js = strip_js(raw)
    defined = set(re.findall(r"\bfunction\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)", raw))
    defined |= set(re.findall(r"\b(?:const|let|var)\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\s*=\s*(?:async\s*)?(?:\([^)]*\)|[A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\s*=>", raw))
    defined |= set(re.findall(r"\b(?:const|let|var)\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\s*=\s*(?:async\s+)?function", raw))
    called = set(re.findall(r"(?<![\w$.'\"])([a-z][A-Za-z_$0-9]*)\s*\(", js))
    unknown = sorted(c for c in called - defined - JS_GLOBALS)
    if unknown:
        fails.append("called but never defined: %s" % unknown)
    else:
        notes.append("every function called is defined")

    # 8. compliance sentinels
    for s in COMPLIANCE_SENTINELS:
        if s not in src:
            fails.append(f"AU compliance sentinel missing from file: {s!r}")

    size = os.path.getsize(path)
    lines = src.count("\n") + (0 if src.endswith("\n") else 1)
    print(f"preflight: {path}  {lines:,} lines  {size:,} bytes")
    for n in notes:
        print("  ok   ", n)
    for f in fails:
        print("  FAIL ", f)
    print("RESULT:", "PASS - safe to publish" if not fails else f"FAIL - {len(fails)} check(s) failed")
    return 0 if not fails else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    args = [a for a in sys.argv[1:] if not a.startswith("--")]
    if not args:
        print(__doc__)
        sys.exit(2)
    sys.exit(main(args[0], strict_tokens="--strict-tokens" in sys.argv))

1505  <div class="phead"><div class="wrap">
1506    <div class="crumb"><a href="#/">Home</a> / Become an Operator</div>
1507    <h1>Become a LaundryKings operator</h1>
1508    <p>Run laundry collections in your own suburb, as your own business, with our customers, brand and systems behind you.</p>
1509  </div></div>
1510  <div class="sec"><div class="wrap">
1511    <div class="g2c" style="gap:3rem;align-items:start">
1512      <div>
1513        <h2 class="h2" style="font-size:var(--fs-5)">What the arrangement is</h2>
1514        <p class="lede" style="margin-bottom:1.2rem">You operate as an independent business with your own ABN. You use your own equipment and premises, choose your own hours, and decide how much work you take on. We route customers to you, handle the booking platform, brand and customer support, and pay you on an agreed revenue share.</p>
1515        <ul class="chk2" style="margin-bottom:1.8rem">
1516          <li>Your own ABN, your own business, your own hours</li>
1517          <li>Your own washing and drying equipment</li>
1518          <li>Jobs routed to you by suburb — no advertising or cold calling</li>
1519          <li>Booking, scheduling, invoicing and payment handled by our platform</li>
1520          <li>Onboarding, training and ongoing support provided</li>
1521          <li>No franchise purchase price and no territory buy-in</li>
1522        </ul>
1523
1524        <h2 class="h2" style="font-size:var(--fs-5)">What you need</h2>
1525        <ul class="chk2" style="margin-bottom:1.8rem">
1526          <li>An active ABN, or willingness to register one</li>
1527          <li>Reliable washing machine and dryer, and space to work</li>
1528          <li>A vehicle for collections and returns</li>
1529          <li>Willingness to complete identity and background screening</li>
1530          <li>Appropriate insurance — we will tell you exactly which policies before you start</li>
1531          <li>A smartphone for the operator app</li>
1532        </ul>
1533
1534        <div class="pnote"><strong>Before you apply, read this.</strong> This is an independent contracting arrangement, not employment. That has real consequences for your tax, superannuation and entitlements, and whether it is the correct classification depends on how the work is actually performed. We will give you the full written agreement to review before you commit, and we recommend you take independent advice on it. We will not pretend this is a decision to make casually.</div>
1535
1536        <h2 class="h2" style="font-size:var(--fs-5);margin-top:2rem">What we cannot tell you yet</h2>
1537        <div class="empty" style="text-align:left">
1538          <h3>Earnings figures</h3>
1539          <p style="margin:0 0 .8rem">We are not publishing income claims. We have not been operating long enough to have real operator earnings data, and quoting a range we cannot evidence would be misleading. Once we have genuine figures from active operators, they will be published here with the basis they were calculated on.</p>
1540          <p style="margin:0">What we can tell you is the revenue share percentage, the expected job volume in your suburb, and the current booking rate. We will give you all three in writing during the application conversation.</p>
1541        </div>
1542      </div>
1543
1544      <div class="fpanel">
1545        <h3>Register your interest</h3>
1546        <p class="fsub">We will be in touch to talk through the arrangement, answer your questions, and send you the agreement to review.</p>

1573<!-- ================================================================
1574     ABOUT
1575     ================================================================ -->
1576<section class="page" id="p-about">
1577  <div class="phead"><div class="wrap">
1578    <div class="crumb"><a href="#/">Home</a> / About</div>
1579    <h1>About LaundryKings</h1>
1580    <p>An Australian-owned laundry collection and delivery business built for South East Queensland.</p>
1581  </div></div>
1582  <div class="sec"><div class="wrap"><div class="prose">
1583    <h2>What we are</h2>
1584    <p>LaundryKings collects your laundry, washes it properly, and brings it back. That is the whole proposition. We are not a laundromat you drive to, and we are not an app that disappears when something goes wrong.</p>
1585    <p>We operate through a network of local operators — independent small businesses running collections in their own suburbs — supported by a central booking, routing and customer service platform. That means the person handling your washing lives near you, and the systems behind them are run properly.</p>
1586
1587    <h2>Why we built it this way</h2>
1588    <p>Two things are broken in this category. Traditional laundries keep hours that working people cannot use, and publish no pricing at all. App-based services solved the convenience problem but treated the people doing the work as disposable, and most of them are gone.</p>
1589    <p>We have built for the middle: published pricing, proper scheduling, and operators who are treated as business partners rather than units of throughput. A round only opens when there is someone good enough to run it.</p>
1590
1591    <h2>How we are different</h2>
1592    <ul>
1593      <li><strong>Published pricing.</strong> Every rate is on this website. You never have to ring to find out what something costs.</li>
1594      <li><strong>Price confirmed before work starts.</strong> Your operator weighs and counts, then confirms the figure. Nothing proceeds until you have it.</li>
1595      <li><strong>Your wash is your wash.</strong> Processed on its own cycle, never combined with another household's items.</li>
1596      <li><strong>We say what we cannot do.</strong> If we are not accredited for something, or not registered for something, we say so on the relevant page rather than letting you find out later.</li>
1597    </ul>
1598
1599    <h2>Coverage</h2>
1600    <p>We are currently building out across Brisbane, Logan and South East Queensland, suburb by suburb. Our <a href="#/coverage" style="text-decoration:underline;color:var(--navy)">coverage page</a> shows where we are live and where we are opening next.</p>
1601
1602    <h2>Company details</h2>
1603    <p>Trading as LaundryKings. Company and ABN details are published in the footer of every page and on our <a href="#/contact" style="text-decoration:underline;color:var(--navy)">contact page</a>.</p>
1604  </div></div></div>
1605</section>
1606

1608     GUARANTEE
1609     ================================================================ -->
1610<section class="page" id="p-guarantee">
1611  <div class="phead"><div class="wrap">
1612    <div class="crumb"><a href="#/">Home</a> / Service Guarantee</div>
1613    <h1>Our service guarantee</h1>
1614    <p>What we promise, what we will do if we get it wrong, and the limits of both — in plain language.</p>
1615  </div></div>
1616  <div class="sec"><div class="wrap"><div class="prose">
1617    <h2>If the wash is not right</h2>
1618    <p>Tell us within <strong>48 hours</strong> of delivery and we will re-wash the affected items at no charge, collected and returned at our cost. If a re-wash will not fix it, we will refund the charge for those items.</p>
1619
1620    <h2>If we are late</h2>
1621    <p>If we miss your agreed return date and have not contacted you in advance to reschedule, the delivery fee for that order is refunded automatically. You do not need to ask.</p>
1622
1623    <h2>If something is damaged or missing</h2>
1624    <p>Report it within <strong>7 days</strong> of delivery. We will investigate using the item count and weight recorded at collection, and respond within 5 business days with an outcome.</p>
1625    <p>Where we are at fault, we will settle the claim. Settlement is based on the item's current value taking reasonable account of age and condition, not its original purchase price. This is standard practice across the industry and we would rather state it here than argue about it later.</p>
1626
1627    <h3>What is not covered</h3>
1628    <ul>
1629      <li>Items where the manufacturer's care label was absent, illegible, or where following it caused the damage</li>
1630      <li>Pre-existing damage, wear or weakness not visible at collection</li>
1631      <li>Colour bleed from items not identified as at-risk at collection</li>
1632      <li>Items left in pockets — we check, but we cannot guarantee we catch everything</li>
1633      <li>Loss or damage to items we specifically advised against processing and you asked us to proceed with anyway</li>
1634    </ul>
1635
1636    <h2>High-value items</h2>
1637    <p>Tell us before collection if any item is worth more than <strong>$250</strong>. We will either handle it under a specific arrangement or tell you we are not the right service for it. Items above this value that were not declared at collection are covered only to the standard limit.</p>
1638
1639    <h2>Insurance</h2>
1640    <p>Our operators are required to hold current public liability cover, and the specific policy type and minimum sum insured are set out in the operator agreement. We will confirm the exact cover in place on request — email us and we will send you the detail rather than a vague assurance.</p>
1641
1642    <h2>Your rights under Australian Consumer Law</h2>
1643    <p>This guarantee sits <strong>on top of</strong> your rights under the Australian Consumer Law. Nothing on this page limits or replaces those rights. Our services come with guarantees that cannot be excluded under the ACL, including that services are provided with due care and skill. If you believe we have failed to meet those guarantees, you have remedies available to you regardless of what this page says.</p>
1644
1645    <h2>How to make a claim</h2>
1646    <p>Contact us through the <a href="#/contact" style="text-decoration:underline;color:var(--navy)">contact page</a> with your order reference, the affected items, and photographs where relevant. We will acknowledge within one business day.</p>
1647  </div></div></div>
1648</section>

1727<section class="page" id="p-privacy">
1728  <div class="phead"><div class="wrap">
1729    <div class="crumb"><a href="#/">Home</a> / Privacy Policy</div>
1730    <h1>Privacy policy</h1>
1731    <p>How we collect, use, store and disclose your personal information.</p>
1732  </div></div>
1733  <div class="sec"><div class="wrap"><div class="prose">
1734    <div class="updated">Draft for legal review — not yet in force. Date of effect to be inserted on approval.</div>
1735    <p>This policy explains how LaundryKings handles personal information in accordance with the <strong>Privacy Act 1988 (Cth)</strong> and the Australian Privacy Principles.</p>
1736
1737    <h2>1. What we collect</h2>
1738    <ul>
1739      <li><strong>Identity and contact details</strong> — name, email, phone number, residential or business address.</li>
1740      <li><strong>Service information</strong> — collection addresses, access instructions, laundry preferences, order history, item counts and weights.</li>
1741      <li><strong>Payment information</strong> — processed by our third-party payment provider. We do not store full card numbers on our systems.</li>
1742      <li><strong>Communications</strong> — enquiries, messages and records of our correspondence with you.</li>
1743      <li><strong>Technical information</strong> — IP address, browser type and pages visited, collected when you use this website.</li>
1744      <li><strong>Operator applicants</strong> — additionally, ABN, equipment details, availability, and the results of identity and background screening.</li>
1745    </ul>
1746
1747    <h2>2. Why we collect it</h2>
1748    <ul>
1749      <li>To provide the service you have booked and to contact you about it</li>
1750      <li>To process payments and issue invoices</li>
1751      <li>To route your job to an operator in your area</li>
1752      <li>To handle enquiries, complaints and claims</li>
1753      <li>To meet our legal and tax record-keeping obligations</li>
1754      <li>To improve our service and plan where we open next</li>
1755    </ul>
1756
1757    <h2>3. Who we share it with</h2>
1758    <p>We disclose personal information only where necessary, to:</p>
1759    <ul>
1760      <li><strong>The operator assigned to your job</strong> — your name, collection address, access instructions and service preferences. Operators are bound by confidentiality obligations in their agreement with us.</li>
1761      <li><strong>Specialist partner cleaners</strong> — where your order includes dry cleaning.</li>
1762      <li><strong>Service providers</strong> — payment processing, SMS and email delivery, cloud hosting and customer support tooling.</li>
1763      <li><strong>Plan managers or support coordinators</strong> — only where you have engaged us through them, and only invoicing and service information.</li>
1764      <li><strong>Law enforcement or regulators</strong> — where we are legally required to do so.</li>
1765    </ul>
1766    <p>We do not sell personal information. We do not disclose it for third-party marketing.</p>
1767
1768    <h2>4. Overseas disclosure</h2>
1769    <p>Some of our technology providers store data outside Australia. Where that is the case we take reasonable steps to ensure the recipient handles the information consistently with the Australian Privacy Principles. The specific providers and their locations will be listed here before this policy takes effect.</p>
1770
1771    <h2>5. Security</h2>
1772    <p>We hold personal information in access-controlled systems and restrict access to those who need it to do their job. No system is perfectly secure. If a data breach occurs that is likely to result in serious harm, we will notify affected individuals and the Office of the Australian Information Commissioner as required under the Notifiable Data Breaches scheme.</p>
1773
1774    <h2>6. Retention</h2>
1775    <p>We keep personal information only as long as needed for the purpose it was collected, or as required by law — including tax and business record-keeping obligations, generally five years. Unsuccessful operator applications are destroyed within 12 months unless you ask us to keep them on file.</p>
1776
1777    <h2>7. Accessing and correcting your information</h2>
1778    <p>You may request access to the personal information we hold about you, and ask us to correct it. Contact us through the <a href="#/contact" style="text-decoration:underline;color:var(--navy)">contact page</a>. We will respond within 30 days. If we refuse access we will tell you why in writing.</p>
1779
1780    <h2>8. Marketing</h2>
1781    <p>We may send you service updates and offers by email or SMS. Every marketing message includes an unsubscribe method. Opting out of marketing does not stop operational messages about a booking you have made.</p>
1782
1783    <h2>9. Cookies</h2>
1784    <p>This website uses cookies and similar technologies for essential functionality and analytics. You can block cookies in your browser, though some parts of the site may not work correctly.</p>
1785
1786    <h2>10. Complaints</h2>
1787    <p>If you believe we have mishandled your personal information, contact us first and we will investigate. If you are not satisfied with our response, you may complain to the Office of the Australian Information Commissioner at oaic.gov.au.</p>
1788
1789    <h2>11. Changes</h2>
1790    <p>We may update this policy. The current version is always published here with its date of effect.</p>
1791  </div></div></div>
1792</section>

1797<section class="page" id="p-terms">
1798  <div class="phead"><div class="wrap">
1799    <div class="crumb"><a href="#/">Home</a> / Terms of Service</div>
1800    <h1>Terms of service</h1>
1801    <p>The terms on which we provide laundry collection, cleaning and delivery services.</p>
1802  </div></div>
1803  <div class="sec"><div class="wrap"><div class="prose">
1804    <div class="updated">Draft for legal review — not yet in force. Date of effect to be inserted on approval.</div>
1805
1806    <h2>1. These terms</h2>
1807    <p>These terms apply when you book any service from LaundryKings. By placing a booking you agree to them. If you do not agree, do not book.</p>
1808
1809    <h2>2. Bookings and quotes</h2>
1810    <p>Prices shown on this website and in the online estimator are <strong>indicative</strong>. Your final price is determined after your items are weighed and counted at collection, and is confirmed to you before any cleaning begins. If you do not accept the confirmed price, your items are returned to you at no charge.</p>
1811
1812    <h2>3. Collection and delivery</h2>
1813    <p>Collection and delivery occur within the window agreed at booking. Where you have authorised unattended collection or delivery, items left at an agreed location are at your risk from the point they are left until they are collected by us, and from the point they are delivered until you retrieve them.</p>
1814    <p>If we cannot access your items at the agreed time through no fault of ours, a failed collection fee may apply. It will be disclosed to you before it is charged.</p>
1815
1816    <h2>4. Your items</h2>
1817    <p>You confirm that items you submit:</p>
1818    <ul>
1819      <li>Are yours, or you are authorised to submit them</li>
1820      <li>Are suitable for the service selected</li>
1821      <li>Do not contain hazardous materials, biohazards, sharps, or contaminated waste</li>
1822      <li>Have been emptied of valuables, cash, keys and electronics</li>
1823    </ul>
1824    <p>We are not liable for items left in pockets. We may refuse to process any item at our discretion, and will tell you why.</p>
1825
1826    <h2>5. Care labels and risk</h2>
1827    <p>We process items in accordance with their manufacturer care labels. Where a label is missing, illegible or incorrect, we process according to reasonable professional judgement and are not liable for resulting damage. Where we assess an item as high-risk we will contact you and proceed only with your instruction, at your risk.</p>
1828
1829    <h2>6. Payment</h2>
1830    <p>Payment is due once your final price is confirmed and before return delivery, unless you hold an approved account. Kings Club memberships are billed in advance on your nominated cycle. We may suspend service on overdue accounts after written notice.</p>
1831
1832    <h2>7. Kings Club</h2>
1833    <p>Kings Club has no minimum term. You may pause or cancel with notice before your next scheduled collection; billing stops from that point. Fees already paid for a completed billing period are not refunded. We may change plan pricing with written notice before your next billing date, and you may cancel rather than accept the change.</p>
1834
1835    <h2>8. Liability and claims</h2>
1836    <p>Claims must be made within the timeframes set out in our <a href="#/guarantee" style="text-decoration:underline;color:var(--navy)">Service Guarantee</a>. Our liability for loss or damage is limited as described there, and is based on an item's current value taking reasonable account of age and condition.</p>
1837    <p>Nothing in these terms excludes, restricts or modifies any guarantee, right or remedy you have under the <strong>Australian Consumer Law</strong> that cannot lawfully be excluded. Where our liability can be limited under the ACL, it is limited to resupplying the service or paying the cost of resupply.</p>
1838
1839    <h2>9. Unclaimed items</h2>
1840    <p>Where we cannot deliver items and cannot contact you, we will hold them for 90 days and make reasonable attempts to reach you. After 90 days we may dispose of or donate them, and recover reasonable storage costs.</p>
1841
1842    <h2>10. Our operators</h2>
1843    <p>Services are performed by independent operators engaged by us. We remain responsible to you for the service under these terms and under the Australian Consumer Law.</p>
1844
1845    <h2>11. Suspension and termination</h2>
1846    <p>We may decline or discontinue service where items are unsuitable, where an account is overdue, or where an operator's safety is at risk. We will tell you why.</p>
1847
1848    <h2>12. Governing law</h2>
1849    <p>These terms are governed by the laws of Queensland, Australia, and the courts of Queensland have jurisdiction.</p>
1850
1851    <h2>13. Changes</h2>
1852    <p>We may update these terms. The version published here at the time you place a booking is the version that applies to it.</p>
1853  </div></div></div>

2430/* ============================================================
2431   LAUNDRYKINGS — APPLICATION
2432   Backend: claude.use("db") — real persistence.
2433   Nothing on this page is fabricated. Prices live in db
2434   config/pricing and are editable without a rebuild.
2435   ============================================================ */
2436
2437/* ---------- DEFAULT PRICING (launch placeholders) ----------
2438   These are LAUNCH ASSUMPTIONS, not confirmed rates.
2439   Basis: benchmarked against comparable AU home-operator
2440   laundry services. Variable: real operator cost per load,
2441   fuel/vehicle cost per stop, and target margin.
2442   Owner can overwrite these in db at config/pricing.        */
2443const DEFAULT_PRICING = {
2444  load: 32.00,          // per ~5kg load, wash & fold
2445  delivery: 13.50,      // collection + return, one flat fee
2446  minimum: 73.50,   // Laundry Lady $70 min +5% — decision 18 Sep 2026
2447  dcJacket: 18.50,
2448  dcDress: 16.50,
2449  dcSuit: 32.00,
2450  bulkDoona: 45.00,
2451  bulkDoonaKing: 75.00,
2452  bulkCurtain: 38.00,
2453  linenSheetSet: 24.00,   // sheet set, per set — large-linen service, not by weight
2454  linenBlanket: 32.00,    // single/double blanket or quilt cover, per item
2455  linenRug: 42.00,        // rug or floor mat, per item, subject to size
2456  ecoAddon: 3.50,
2457  // Kings Club, +30% on the launch rate, effective 19 Sep 2026
2458  plan1: 63.70, plan2: 89.70, plan3: 115.70
2459};
2460let PX = Object.assign({}, DEFAULT_PRICING);
2461let DB = null, USER = null, CAN_EDIT = false;
2462
2463const money = n => { const v = Number(n) || 0; return '$' + (Math.abs(v - Math.round(v)) < 0.005 ? String(Math.round(v)) : v.toFixed(2)); };

2501function setTitle(id){

2506/* ---------- SCROLL REVEAL ----------
2507   Runs only where the viewer has not asked for reduced motion, and only once
2508   per element. Everything is visible without it. */
2509(() => {
2510  if (!window.matchMedia || window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches) return;
2511})();
2512function wireReveal(){
2513  if (!window.IntersectionObserver) return;
2514  if (window.matchMedia && window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches) return;
2515  const targets = document.querySelectorAll('.page.live .sec > .wrap > *, .page.live .trust-c, .page.live .card');
2516  if (!targets.length) return;
2517  const io = new IntersectionObserver(es => {
2518    es.forEach(e => { if (e.isIntersecting){ e.target.classList.add('in'); io.unobserve(e.target); } });
2519  }, { rootMargin: '0px 0px -8% 0px', threshold: 0.05 });
2520  targets.forEach(t => { t.classList.add('reveal'); io.observe(t); });
2521}

2523function route(){
2524  const h = (location.hash || '#/').replace('#/','').split('?')[0] || 'home';
2525  const id = PAGES.includes(h) ? h : 'home';
2526  document.querySelectorAll('.page').forEach(p => p.classList.remove('live'));
2527  const el = document.getElementById('p-' + id);
2528  if (el) el.classList.add('live');
2529  document.querySelectorAll('[data-nav]').forEach(a => a.classList.toggle('on', a.dataset.nav === id));
2530  window.scrollTo(0,0);
2531  document.getElementById('mob').classList.remove('open');
2532  setTitle(id);
2533  try { wireReveal(); } catch(e){}
2534  if (id === 'dash') loadDash();
2535  try { appRoute(id); } catch(e){}
2536}
2537window.addEventListener('hashchange', route);

2545    raf = requestAnimationFrame(() => { nav.classList.toggle('scrolled', window.scrollY > 4); raf = null; });
2546  };
2547  window.addEventListener('scroll', onScroll, { passive: true });
2548})();

2569/* ---------- QUOTE CALCULATOR ---------- */
2570const C = { a: 2, b: 0 };
2571document.querySelectorAll('[data-step]').forEach(btn => {
2572  btn.onclick = () => {
2573    const [k, d] = btn.dataset.step.split(',');
2574    C[k] = Math.max(k === 'a' ? 1 : 0, C[k] + Number(d));
2575    const numEl = document.getElementById('num' + k.toUpperCase());
2576    numEl.textContent = C[k];
2577    numEl.classList.remove('bump'); void numEl.offsetWidth; numEl.classList.add('bump');
2578    calc();
2579  };
2580});
2581document.getElementById('qSvc').onchange = calc;

2583function calc(){
2584  const svc = document.getElementById('qSvc').value;
2585  const lA = document.getElementById('lblA');
2586  let sub = 0, lines = '';
2587
2588  if (svc === 'comm'){
2589    document.getElementById('qBreak').innerHTML =
2590      '<p style="display:block;color:var(--body)">Commercial and linen work is quoted per site based on volume, frequency and soil level. Use the commercial enquiry form for a written quote.</p>';
2591    document.getElementById('qTotal').textContent = 'By quote';
2592    lA.textContent = 'Not applicable';
2593    return;
2594  }
2595
2596  if (svc === 'wf'){
2597    lA.textContent = 'Loads (approx. 5 kg each)';
2598    sub = C.a * PX.load;
2599    lines = row(C.a + ' × wash & fold load' + (C.a>1?'s':''), sub);
2600  } else if (svc === 'linen'){
2601    lA.textContent = 'Items (sheet sets, blankets or rugs)';
2602    sub = C.a * PX.linenSheetSet;
2603    lines = row(C.a + ' × linen item at sheet set rate', sub);
2604    lines += '<p style="font-size:var(--fs-badge);color:var(--mute);margin:.1rem 0 0">Sheet set ' + money(PX.linenSheetSet) + ' · Blanket ' + money(PX.linenBlanket) + ' · Rug ' + money(PX.linenRug) + ' — exact price confirmed before work begins</p>';
2605  } else if (svc === 'dc'){
2606    lA.textContent = 'Garments (mixed average)';
2607    sub = C.a * PX.dcDress;
2608    lines = row(C.a + ' × garment' + (C.a>1?'s':'') + ' (indicative average)', sub);
2609  }
2610
2611  lines += row('Collection & return', PX.delivery);
2612  sub += PX.delivery;
2613  const tot = Math.max(PX.minimum, sub);
2614  if (tot > sub + 0.001) lines += row('Minimum order adjustment', tot - sub);
2615  lines += '<p class="tot"><span>Estimated total</span><span>' + money(tot) + '</span></p>';
2616  document.getElementById('qBreak').innerHTML = lines;
2617  document.getElementById('qTotal').textContent = money(tot);
2618}
2619const row = (l, v) => '<p><span>' + l + '</span><span>' + money(v) + '</span></p>';

2624function paintHomeV4(){
2625  // hero price teaser — 2 loads wash & fold + delivery, live from PX
2626  const twoLoads = PX.load * 2;
2627  const del = PX.delivery;
2628  const sub = twoLoads + del;
2629  const tot = Math.max(PX.minimum, sub);
2630  const set = (id,v)=>{ const e=document.getElementById(id); if(e) e.textContent = money(v); };
2631  set('hpLoads', twoLoads);
2632  set('hpDel', del);
2633  set('hpTot', tot);
2634
2635  // Kings Club compare bar — prices from PX so they track config/pricing overrides
2636  const adhoc = PX.load * 2 + PX.delivery;
2637  const kcEl = document.getElementById('kcSaveM');
2638  if(kcEl) kcEl.innerHTML = 'Two ad-hoc loads a week ≈ ' + money(adhoc) + ' · Kings Club Solo = ' + money(PX.plan1) + '/week.<br><b>Save around ' + money(Math.round(adhoc - PX.plan1)) + ' a week compared with two ad-hoc loads</b> — no lock-in, pause any time.';
2639
2640  // live suburb count
2641  const n = Object.keys(LIVE_PC).length;
2642  const cc = document.getElementById('covCount');
2643  if(cc) cc.textContent = n;
2644
2645  // live coverage chips (home band 6)
2646  const host = document.getElementById('covLiveChips');
2647  if(host){
2648    host.innerHTML = Object.entries(LIVE_PC)
2649      .sort((a,b)=>a[1].localeCompare(b[1]))
2650      .map(([pc,nm])=>`<button class="cov-chip" data-covpc="${pc}"><span class="d"></span>${nm}</button>`)
2651      .join('');
2652    host.querySelectorAll('[data-covpc]').forEach(b=>b.onclick=()=>{
2653      const f=document.getElementById('covPc');
2654      if(f){ f.value=b.dataset.covpc; document.getElementById('covGo').click();
2655        f.scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth',block:'center'}); }
2656    });
2657  }
2658}
